## 04 · Opening up · 1 Look at the chart showing some general categories of behavior...

1 Look at the chart showing some general categories of behavior.
Then work in pairs and think of a few specific examples of
behavior for each category

annoying behavior: talking loudly in public places, interrupting
others in conversations, invadingpersonal space, complaining
endlessly, spreading rumors about others, often being late for class,
using offensive language

cooperative behavior: helping people on your team, contributing
ideas to your group, participating actively in discussions, listening
carefully to your team members, fulfilling group tasks on time

violent behavior: verbal or physical threats, physical fighting,
assaulting / punching / kicking / Hitting others, bullying,
cyberbullying

polite behavior: expressing-thanks to people who helped you,
being punctual, holding the door open for others, offering your
seat to someone in need, listening attentively to and showing
interest in what others have to say

## 22 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

Scripts

Moral virtues hold a central position in;Confucianism, and are
considered the basic principles of human behavior and the
foundation for a just and harmonious society. The Five Constant
Virtues, also known as the Five Constants or Wuchang,
advocated by Confucianism, are ren, yi, li, zhi, and xin.

Ren, or benevolence, is the most important virtue treasured by

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 23 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

（整屏红字，参考答案）

Confucianism. It means showing genuine concern and being kind and caring toward others. According to Confucius, if we all practice ren, we could have a peaceful and happy society.

Yi, or righteousness, is also regarded as an important virtue in Confucian thought. It emphasizes the pursuit of justice and moral integrity. According to Confucius, righteousness is essential for

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 24 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

achieving personal goodness, as well as maintaining social fairness and justice.

Li, or propriety, is the third important virtue encouraged by Confucianism. It is about proper conduct and behavior in different social settings. In Confucian ethics, if we are polite and observe proper etiquette toward others, we can build relationships

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 25 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

of mutual respect and trust.

Zhi, or wisdom, is the fourth essential virtue of Confucianism. It highlights the importance of knowledge, learning, and critical thinking. In Confucianism, the pursuit of wisdom and education plays a key role in achieving personal growth and social advancement.

## 26 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

（整屏红字，参考答案）

Xin, or faithfulness, is also an essential virtue promoted by Confucianism. It involves being loyal, honest, and trustworthy in all aspects of life. In Confucian philosophy, faithfulness is indispensable in building strong personal relationships and long-term social stability.

The Five Constant Virtues were developed over two thousand

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 27 · Taking in · Listening to China · Scripts

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

years ago, yet they remain as important today. By cultivating moral virtues such as ren, yi, zhi, and xin, individuals can lead an ethical life and contribute positively to society.

## 28 · Taking in · Listening to China · After you listen

After you listen

4 Work in pairs and discuss the questions.

1 Why do you think the Five Constant Virtues proposed by Confucianism over 2,000 years ago remain relevant and important to our society today?

（红笔作答）

There are several reasons why the Five Constant Virtues of Confucianism remain relevant and important today. Firstly, these virtues embody fundamental moral and ethical values.

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 29 · Taking in · Listening to China · After you listen

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

By actively practicing them, individuals can nurture positive qualities within themselves and cultivate stronger personal relationships. This, in turn, contributes to a more harmonious and cohesive society.

Secondly, embracing these virtues not only helps preserve and uphold our cultural heritage but also fosters a strong sense of

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 30 · Taking in · Listening to China · After you listen

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

cultural identity in ourselves. By adhering to these values, we contribute to the continuity of our cultural traditions and reinforce the significance of our cultural values.

Thirdly, the principles underlying these virtues resonate with values that can be found across different cultures. These virtues have the power to foster intercultural understanding, dialogue, and

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 31 · Taking in · Listening to China · After you listen

（接上屏）

（整屏红字，参考答案）

cooperation, particularly in our increasingly interconnected world.

Therefore, through recognizing the enduring relevance of the Five Constant Virtues advocated by Confucianism, we can actively incorporate them into our lives, thereby promoting personal growth, social harmony, and a more inclusive and compassionate global community.

1 How many different types of handshakes do you know? What causes these variations?

（红笔作答）

I am familiar with two different types of handshakes. In China, when people shake hands, they take hold of each other's right hand strongly (or softly). In the Republic of Korea, people often have a two-handed handshake, and they do so either with both hands

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 36 · Taking in · Viewing world · Before you view

（接上屏）

（红笔作答）

clasping the other person's hands, with the left hand on their right wrist or elbow. I think this is mainly due to cultural differences.

2 In what circumstances do people usually shake hands? What are the reasons behind this social gesture?

## 37 · Taking in · Viewing world · Before you view

（整屏红字，参考答案）

People shake hands in various circumstances, such as in business settings, or when meeting someone for the first time, greeting a friend after not seeing each other for a long time, offering congratulations, or saying goodbye.

People shake hands as a way of confirming friendship, showing respect, establishing trust, building connection, or demonstrating professionalism and confidence.

## 53 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

Desmond Morris:

Back in the late 1960s, I was sitting in this very restaurant on the island of Malta, talking to my publisher. I drew his attention to the fact that, over the other side of the road, there were two men who were gesticulating in a particular way. The way they were holding their palms to one side was fascinating me, and he said,

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 54 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

（接上屏）

"You know, you look at people the way that a bird-watcher looks at birds," and I said, "Yeah, I suppose you could call me a 'man-watcher'."

As soon as I said it, it was as if I'd fired a starting gun on a major new project, one that was to engross me for many years to come and take me to over 60 different countries. I was going to do for

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 55 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

（接上屏）

actions what dictionary makers had done for words. I began making huge charts naming every facial expression, every gesticulation, every movement, (and) every posture. I kept at it for month after month.

One of the first problems I encountered was that even the simplest human action, such as the handshake, has countless variations.

## 56 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

Sometimes it's reduced to a mere palm touch, as with these Masai elders in East Africa. But in other countries, it becomes more elaborate. In Mali in West Africa, the handshaker briefly touches his own forearm as the palms clasp.

In Morocco, the handshakers kiss one another's hands at the same time as clasping them. And in Turkiye, these Kurdish farmers

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 57 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

（接上屏）

have taken this simple action and converted it into what amounts to a minor ritual. It's the local rule that they can't start bargaining until they're shaking hands, and they have to keep on doing so until the deal is struck.

The essential feature of handshaking is that it's an egalitarian act. Regardless of their social standing, the two people involved are

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 58 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

（接上屏）

momentarily performing identical actions.

Despite their variations, all these greetings have one thing in common: They're all fine-tuned to the precise context in which they occur.

Because a single message is given in a different way in different countries - the crazy sign: How do you say to somebody, "You're

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 59 · Taking in · Viewing world cultures · Scripts

## 113 · Speaking out · Public speaking · Public speaking skills · Persuasive speeches - Methods of persuasion

Persuasive speeches - Methods of persuasion

Persuasive speeches are speeches with which the speaker tries to
influence the listeners' attitudes, opinions, values, or beliefs. The
goal of a persuasive speech is to defend an idea or inspire the
listeners to take action.

In order to make your persuasion successful, it is important for you
to learn some common methods of persuasion.

（注：本屏正文下方检测到零散浅红色笔迹（多为细短线，分布于各行下缘），以及屏底 x≈688-706 / y≈509 处一道极小红笔划痕；内容均无法辨认。屏内另有水印残字「满」已剔除。）

## 114 · Speaking out · Public speaking · Public speaking skills · Building credibility

One common method of persuasion is to'build your credibility.

Credibility refers to the audience's perception of your qualifications
on a given topic. It is affected mainly by two factors: competence
and character. Therefore, to build your credibility, you can mention
to your audience your expertise or special experience with the topic.
You can also enhance your credibility by delivering the speech in a

（屏底截断，接下屏）

（接上屏）

poised and confident manner.

Using evidence

Evidence refers to supporting materials such as facts, examples,
statistics, and testimony. When you prove an idea, you should use
evidence or supporting materials from credible sources. Your
evidence should be specific, up-to-date, unbiased, and representative.

（注：本屏有零散浅红笔迹，以及屏底 x≈674-707 与 x≈770-794 / y≈501 处两道极小红笔划痕，内容均无法辨认。）

## 116 · Speaking out · Public speaking · Public speaking skills · Reasoning · Appealing to emotions

Reasoning

Reasoning is a process of forming conclusions, inferences, or
judgments on the basis of evidence. To make your conclusions
sound and valid, you must reason logically and avoid making hasty
generalizations.

Appealing to emotions

Emotional appeals are intended to arouse a variety of feelings in the

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 117 · Speaking out · Public speaking · Public speaking skills · Appealing to emotions

（接上屏）

audience, such as sympathy, pride, admiration, anger, fear, or guilt.
When the audience's emotions are touched, they will more likely
agree with your point of view or be inspired to take action.

When you prepare for a persuasive speech, you should make active
use of these methods so that your speech will be well-supported and
your arguments convincing.

Don't stay up late

How many hours do you sleep every day? What time do you go to
bed every night? If your answers are no more than six hours a day
and you don't go to bed until midnight, /I will not be surprised at all.
Staying up late seems very common-among college students.
However, this is a bad habit. As a student who used to have this
problem, but has overcome it and is enjoying many of the benefits
brought by the change, today I'd like to speak to you about why
staying up late can be harmful and encourage you to seek a change,

（屏底截断，接下屏）

too. I will first talk about the problems caused by staying up late, and
then give you some tips on how-to ensure you go to bed earlier.

Let's start with the problems causedby staying up late.

First, staying up late can be bad for our immune system. Studies
show that when healthy people-fail to go to sleep early, their bodies
become more susceptible to infections. This is because from 10 to 12
at night, our bodies undergo a process of detoxification (解毒). This
process must happen when the body is in a state of deep relaxation.

## 122 · Speaking out · Public speaking

If we are still working during 10 to. 12 at night, it will harm our
health. Every hour of sleep we get before midnight is more beneficial
than the hours after midnight.

Second, staying up late cani have a negative impact on our mental
performance. Without enough-sleep, -we lose our ability to
concentrate and think actively. A study conducted at the University
of California, Berkeley, revealed that just one night of poor sleep can
lead to impaired decision-making abilities and reduced focus.
Another study conducted at the University of Pennsylvania

and ensure sufficient sleep every night.

Third, staying up late causes skin damage. According to experts in

From what I've talked about, I'm-sure most of you now well
understand the harmful effects of staying up late. But you may

to offer.

## 152 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

Do you realize that every behavior we exhibit serves some type of communicative function? Our actions and words convey meaning to others, whether we intend them to or not. Understanding the communicative function of behavior can help us better understand ourselves and those around us.

Behavior can be divided into two types: verbal and nonverbal.

## 153 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

Verbal behaviors include speaking, writing, and even texting or emailing. Nonverbal behaviors include body language, gestures, facial expressions, and tone of voice.

When we engage in verbal behavior, we communicate our thoughts, feelings, and intentions directly through language. We use words to convey information, make requests, and express

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 154 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

（接上屏）

emotions. However, it's important to remember that what we say isn't always what we mean. For example, sarcasm or irony can change the meaning of our words entirely. Context also affects how our words are interpreted.

Nonverbal behaviors can often be more complex than verbal ones. They can express emotions, convey social status, and

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 155 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

（接上屏）

communicate messages that we may not be fully conscious of. For example, our posture, eye contact, and facial expressions can all communicate our level of confidence, engagement, and interest in a conversation. Even our clothing and hairstyle can communicate something about our personality and identity.

Another aspect of nonverbal behavior is paralanguage, which

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 156 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

（接上屏）

refers to the way we use our voice to communicate. This includes intonation, pitch, pace, and volume. The way we say something can convey different messages. Saying "I'm fine" in a cheerful tone can convey happiness, while saying it in a flat or hesitant tone can express the opposite.

It should also be recognized that behavior doesn't occur in

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 157 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

（接上屏）

isolation. Our actions are influenced by our surroundings and cultural background. This means that what is considered appropriate behavior in one context may not be so in another. For instance, a hug is a common greeting in many Western cultures, but may be considered inappropriate or even offensive in other parts of the world.

## 158 · Further listening · Lecture 1 · Scripts

Our behavior, whether verbal or nonverbal, plays an important role in conveying meaning. By being aware of our own behavior and how it is understood by others, we can tailor our words and actions to better meet our goals. Similarly, by paying attention to the behavior of others, we can gain insight into their thoughts and feelings, and better understand their perspectives.

## 163 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

Habits are powerful foundations for our lives. Research shows that they influence around 45 percent of our daily activities. However, adopting new behaviors into our lifestyle can be a major struggle, especially if we're trying to alter unhealthy habits. Fortunately, one reputable American psychologist has some great advice to offer. He suggests that if we're unable to implement

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 164 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

new behaviors consistently, it's not always due to a lack of motivation or willpower; it's because we're taking an improper approach. He recommends that, to achieve a desired goal, we should make small, gradual behavioral adjustments instead of immediately jumping into significant changes. This approach can be applied in many aspects of our life.

## 165 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

Take physical health as an example. We often set goals to lose weight or improve our fitness level, but the thought of following a strict diet or committing to a strict exercise routine can be overwhelming. Instead, we can start by making small, sustainable changes, such as starting the day with a healthy breakfast, taking a short walk in the morning, or doing a few minutes of stretching

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 166 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

before bed. Over time, these small changes will build up and lead to significant improvements in our overall health and wellbeing.

Similarly, in our careers, we often set ambitious goals for ourselves. We aspire to earn promotions, gain recognition for our work, or pursue new opportunities for career development. However, the path to achieving these goals is often long and filled

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 167 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

with uncertainties. To avoid becoming overwhelmed, you should focus on the small steps needed to get there. This might include attending workshops to acquire new skills or knowledge, taking on new responsibilities at work, or seeking out mentors who can provide guidance and support.

When it comes to personal relationships, small steps can have a

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 168 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

big impact as well. Building strong relationships requires patience, understanding, and consistent effort. By committing to small, meaningful gestures - like expressing gratitude, showing empathy, or simply taking the time to actively listen - we can strengthen our connections with others and deepen our sense of belonging.

We're often unprepared for how much time and effort it will take

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 169 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

to establish a new behavior. Trying to make big changes all at once leads to exhaustion and decreased motivation. The "small steps" approach celebrates small successes. By breaking down our objectives into manageable tasks and building healthy habits into our daily routines, we can establish a foundation for lasting change.

## 11 · Taking in · Listening to China · Listening skills · Organizing your notes

Taking in — Listening to China — Listening skills

Organizing your notes

When taking notes during listening, it is important to know how to
organize your notes clearly and coherently. One way to organize
your notes is to divide them into sections and subsections
according to the main points and subpoints the speaker talks about.
The specific sections and subsections may vary according to the

（屏底截断，接下屏）

## 12 · Taking in · Listening to China · Listening skills · Organizing your notes

（接上屏）

genre (体裁), topic, or content of thefistening material.
For example, if the speaker introduces. a'festival or a holiday,
you may hear such things as the date, theme, origin or history,
and traditions or customs of the celebration. To capture these
kinds of information, you may take notes containing the
following parts:

The Chongyang Festival, also referred to as the Double Ninth
Festival, falls on the ninth' day'of the ninth month in the Chinese
lunar calendar. The number nine-is regarded as yang in Chinese
culture, which represents positive forces, life, and brightness,
hence the festival is given the name "Chongyang". In addition,
the word "nine" shares the same pronunciation as the word for

meanings. In 1989, the Chinese government set it as Senior's Day

cultural meanings are important components of Chinese culture.

## 120 · Speaking out / Public speaking

（整屏红字，参考答案）

Is the value of a work of art determined by its price? This is a
question that many of us have thought about at one time or another.
In my opinion, the value of an art pieeeis not measured by how
much money it can fetch in'the market. Instead, it is determined by
three more important factors?its meaning, its impact, and its
originality.
First, the meaning of an artwork. Art is a form of expression that
communicates something beyond words. It conveys emotions, ideas,
messages, stories, or visions that resonate with us on a deeper level.

（题 1 范文即兴演讲的开头。）

## 121 · Speaking out / Public speaking

（整屏红字，参考答案）

It makes us feel joy, sadness, anger, or awe. It makes us think
critically, creatively, or differently: Thus, the meaning of a work of
art is what is truly important and what gives it value.
Second, the impact of an artwork. Art is'riot just something that we
look at or listen to, but something.that affects us and changes us. The
famous French artist Edgar Degas once said, "Art is not what you see,
but what you make others see." This means art inspires people to
think, to act, to create, to learn, or to grow. It influences our opinions,
values, and beliefs. It heals wounds, bridges gaps, and builds

（屏底截断，接下屏）

copy or imitate to sell for a'certain price,but something that we use
to invent, discover, or express:new-ideas. It is a manifestation of our
creativity and imagination. It challenges our conventions and
expectations. It stands for our uniqueness and diversity. Therefore,
the true value of an artwork does not lie in its purchase price, but in
whether it inspires us to explore new possibilities and potentials

portray her as a celebration of their home's completion and the
birth of their second son. What is it about this painting that has
created such a lasting impact on the art world?

Identifying supporting details

When a speaker expresses an'opinion or'an idea, they often
provide some supporting details.to-explain, clarify, or illustrate it.
Therefore, supporting details guide us to a full understanding of
the speaker's opinion or idea, and it is very important to learn to
identify them.

（本屏为听力技能说明页的引言段。红色笔画 1 处（x=0..313 y=116..139，n=1112），为页眉 "Listening to China" 的红笔高亮底纹，非答案。）

## 86 · Taking in · Watching street interviews

Taking
in
Watching street interviews

5 Work in pairs and discuss the questions.

1 What do you think is the relationship between money and happiness?

**红笔答案：In my opinion, money doesn't necessarily bring happiness. There are lots of rich people in the world who live unhappy lives. Happiness is just a state of mind, and it mainly depends on how you look at people and things. However, having money sometimes does make us feel a little happier because it can help us get what we want more easily.**

## 03 · Opening up · 1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

Opening up

1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

The more that you read, the more things you will know. The more
that you learn, the more places you'll go.

Theodor Seuss Geisel (American author and cartoonist)

No tears in the writer, no tears in the reader. No surprise in the
writer, no surprise in the reader.

Robert Frost (American poet)

Having pored over ten thousand volumes, my writing brush seems
to work like magic.

—Du Fu (Chinese poet)

## 04 · Opening up · 1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

Openingup

1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

1 What do the quotes mean?

Each of the three quotes expresses a particular perspective on
human experiences as either a reader or a writer. The first one
talks about the importance of reading, saying that reading can
increase our knowledge and broaden our horizons. The second one
emphasizes that a writer must be emotionally engaged in their
work to evoke an emotional response from the reader. The third
one, which is a famous Chinese quote, means that if one reads
extensively, they will gain the knowledge and wisdom to write
skillfully.

（页眉 "Openingup" 中间缺空格，OCR 如此。"1 What do the quotes mean?" 之下的整段答语为红字印刷体参考答案，正文逐字保留。）

## 05 · Opening up · 1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

Opening up

1 Read the quotes. Then work in pairs and discuss the questions.

2 Which quote do you like best? Why?

I like the first quote best. It summarizes the benefits of reading
precisely and concisely. Reading enables us to learn about things
we may be unable to see or experience in person. It provides a
channel through which we get to learn more about life and the
world. For example, we may not be able to travel to many places
on earth, but we can certainly learn about them through reading.
This quote motivates me to read more in the future.

（"2 Which quote do you like best? Why?" 之下的整段答语为红字印刷体参考答案，正文逐字保留。）

Listening for comparisons and contrasts

Speakers often compare and contrast objects, ideas, processes, or
concepts to help describe and explain something. A comparison is
used to show similarities between two or more things, while a
contrast is used to demonstrate differences between them.
Understanding comparisons and contrasts can help you analyze

technique called block printing. Unlike in moveable type printing,
which has individual characters as separate pieces, in the block
printing process, characters of an entire page are carved out to
make one big block or printing plate. Each block can only be used
for a specific page of a particular book, and a single mistake in
carving could ruin the whole block, making the process very
inefficient and costly.

The term "Victorian literature' refers to English literature written
during the reign of Queen Victoria, from 1837 to 1901. Although
this seems like a short period of time compared to other literary
eras, it is often considered the “"Golden Age of English Literature".
During this time, the novel became the leading literary genre.
While the form of novel had been in use previously, it was during

（屏底截断，接下屏。本屏起为 Lecture 2 听力原文。引号弯直混排为 OCR 形态（"literature'" 行末作单引号、"Golden 行首作弯直双引号叠读），原样保留。本屏未见红笔答案。）

（接上屏）

the Victorian era that it became a key part of English literature.
Victorian novels are famously long. Both printing technology and
literacy improved in this period, creating an increased interest in
long-form fiction. Novels were often printed serially - in sections
over a series of months or years - before being compiled into
complete editions. The serial format helped create excitement and

（屏底截断，接下屏。本屏未见红笔答案。）

anticipation among readers, leading to the greater popularity of
long novels. Charles Dickens is particularly remembered for the
buzz generated by his serialized novels like Oliver Twist published
from 1837 to 1839 and A Tale of Two Cities published in 1859.
Other famous novelists of the period include William Makepeace
Thackeray, the Bronte sisters, Thomas Hardy, and George Eliot.

（屏底截断，接下屏。"Bronte sisters" 原图疑作 "Brontë"（分音符被 OCR 丢失），无法从 OCR 确证，原样保留。本屏未见红笔答案。）

## 163 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

Further listening
Lecture 2
Scripts

The dominant style of Victorian novels was realism. Following
the Romantic period in literature, writers of the Victorian age
turned to focus on reality. They directed their focus toward the
lives of everyday people, individual struggles, and social issues.
Their works reflected transformations in English society in many
areas, from science and technology to class structure and politics.

（屏底截断，接下屏。本屏未见红笔答案。）

## 164 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

Further listening
Lecture 2
Scripts

During the Victorian era, a new genre of literature arose -
children's literature. Although children had always been an
audience for certain stories and tales, authors had not written
commercially to entertain child readers until the Victorian age. As
literacy began to improve and social programs fought to protect
children and ban child labor, children became increasingly popular
as a commercial focus, leading to a boom in children's literature.

（屏底截断，接下屏。本屏未见红笔答案。）

## 165 · Further listening · Lecture 2 · Scripts

（接上屏）

Further listening
Lecture 2
Scripts

Lewis Carroll's Alice's Adventures in Wonderland, published in
1865, was one of the earliest English novels to tap into this new
market, and certainly the most popular. The illustrations and
realistically childish perspective of the book drew a lot of child
readers. Its success inspired many other such novels including
1877's Black Beauty by Anna Sewell and 1899's The Story of the
Treasure Seekers by Edith Nesbit.

（Lecture 2 的 Scripts 至此结束。OCR 作 "TreasureSeekers byEdith"，词间空格被吞，已还原为 "Treasure Seekers by Edith"。本屏未见红笔答案。）
